ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u3	Thank you, Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, today we are called upon to vote on a bill with social importance and national implications. Under the draft law on birth allowance and other provisions, the Government of New Democracy legislates what it has announced and adopts for the first time a birth allowance. One - one - the election promises of the New Democracy Government are being made. The bills that come after each other demonstrate the government's readiness to form a new reality for our country, bills that clearly identify our different approaches and political startings, bills that strip opposition politically, bills that the Greek people understand their essence and accept them positively. So today the bill of birth allowance and other provisions, which we are discussing, is another example of our policy. It detracts from the myth that social sensitivity and social care are the privileges of the Left. This draft law has eyes on the whole of society. We will all agree in this Chamber that the state taking initiatives for the immediate development and implementation of demographic policy measures, in all the context of the country's budgetary possibilities, is more than necessary. It's urgent. It's a national case. In a country where infertileness is a huge problem, which the next generations will find before them, we are coming today to give an extra incentive for new couples. This bill, ladies and gentlemen, is structured at two levels. In the first part, we regulate the grant of the allowance. We are introducing for the first time the 2,000 euro birth allowance for every child born in Greece from 1-1-2020, while respecting and fulfilling all the conditions laid down in this bill. If we look at it from a constitutional point of view, the grant of the allowance is a manifestation of the care of the State for the Greek family, which is guaranteed by a constitutional provision in the provision of Article 21(1) of our Constitution, as well as within the same constitutional framework, in accordance with paragraph 5 of that article, the state's obligation is to design and implement a good demographic policy, as well as to take all necessary measures. The mother of the child will generally be the beneficiary receiving the allowance. However, the allowance will also be received by the father who is diligent of the child and who is permanently and legally resident in the country but also by anyone who exercises custody upon the relevant judicial decision. We pay the allowance in two instalments equal to the first instalment on the last working day of the following month from the month of application and the second six months after birth of the child. Within a more general spirit of the present Government to modernise procedures, we make the application for the birth allowance possible directly from the maternity hospital, by digital application, and we introduce to the public administration a new automated procedure for issuing a registration number and the child's social security number in the registration act of that birth. We're in 2020. No more trouble in the tail. No more bureaucracy. We have said this on several occasions. We take seriously the requirement of the times for digital transformation of all services. We are making the digitisation, simplification and automation as much as possible. We thus relieve public administration of bureaucracy and citizens of lengthy procedures. We have said that we will improve the daily lives of the citizens and step by step. You have repeatedly said, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, that you welcome us in the policy of allowances which we have been appalled so far in the run-up to the elections. The New Democracy has never been and it is not against social welfare, no matter how much the Left wants to rob it, and we prove this with the bills that we bring to Parliament to vote, bills based on the social interest of our country and its development. On the contrary, we welcome you to development policy, investment policy, sound financial planning and tax relief, implemented by the Government of New Democracy and Kyriakos Mitsotakis. We are therefore giving birth allowance where the equivalent family income does not exceed EUR 40,000 annually. 99%, that is, citizens are entitled to the allowance. We cover the vast majority of families. A very small percentage of society as a whole will not receive it. They are the most prosperous, the ones for whom the economy is not a proven problem for bringing a child into the world. Ladies and gentlemen, the beneficiary of the allowance should have a legal and permanent residence on the Greek territory, as well as one of the five properties: the Greek citizen, the indigenous foreigner, who has a special identity card of a homogeneous nature, the citizen of a Member State of the European Union, the citizen of a State belonging to the European Economic Area or citizen of the Swiss Confederation and the citizen of a third country with a previous 12-year stay in the country before the child's birth year. We value the twelve-year stay as a full-time and capable, so that a strong living bond with the country has proved through constant contact with Greek culture and consciousness. However, we exclude children born in Greece in 2020, 2021, 2022 and 2023, since their mother as a third-country citizen in this case has been residing permanently, legally and consistently on Greek territory since 2012. We are setting the beneficiaries of the allowance a legal framework, in accordance with European regulations and constitutional values, and we are giving the allowance to Greeks and to those who are inextricably linked to the country, nothing more, nothing less. We reserve, ladies and gentlemen, not counting on the amount of the allowance to be paid to the total annual income. The allowance will not be taxed or subject to fees, contributions and any other form of bookings. It will not be confiscated and it will not be offset by any debts, whether to the public and public bodies or to private legal persons. No bank will retain one euro from its birth allowance for claims and debts. In the second part of the bill, ladies and gentlemen, we are reforming and updating four but basic allowances. The housing allowance, the child's allowance, the allowance of mountainous and disadvantaged areas and the minimum guaranteed income. We add, as beneficiaries of the child's privilege, citizens of third countries protected by rules both international and European law, as these rules have been incorporated into Greek law. However, we consider that the citizens of third countries who are not under special regime the previous compulsory 12 years of residence in the country to be an adequate compulsory period of time. We are raising the limit from five to twelve years for all the benefits of the beneficiary citizens from third countries that are in the country. We have been doing it for twelve years, because twelve is a full-time school course at school, and we think it is a good time to develop a strong link between a third-country citizen and our country. However, we ensure that the child and the housing allowance continue to be granted to the already beneficiaries. Anyone who has received these benefits so far will continue to receive them properly. Then, for the housing allowance, we define the equal treatment of the seven categories of beneficiaries with the Greek citizens and we introduce as a criterion the granting of a child's and a housing allowance corresponding to the need for children to be trained during compulsory education. As a student, not only do we mean the registration of this but also the actual schooling of the child during the school year. A great deal has been said about the condition of compulsory actual schooling for the allowances which this draft law touches, and because Greek citizens are watching us at the moment it is important to clarify some things so that there is no confusion. Unfortunately, it is part of your policy, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, to distort things. So no allowance cutbacks are hidden behind the association with the school. We don't work punishingly. Honourable would be a state that keeps its children excluded from school. If a child has problems in learning and needs to repeat class, then his parents normally get the allowance. If a child is absent for medical reasons and for a long time and stays absent, then his parents normally take the allowance and the housing and the child and the minimum guaranteed income. At the core of our policy is the child. We want to combat the unjustified absence and the school leak. With the help of these provisions on actual schooling, we seek to reduce the unjustified abstention of minor pupils from their school duties and to strengthen parental care. Stop hypocrisy. We have no memory of goldfish, neither of us nor of the Greek people. You have a government past and it's bad. Continuing with the rest of the bill, ladies and gentlemen, we are making the previous assent of the Minister of Labour and Social Affairs of the Minister of Labour and Social Affairs for the adoption of any decision to transfer or post employees from the Social Welfare Centres and other privileged structures to the issuing of the Agency. When we received the Ministry of Social Welfare and Solidarity, the welfare centres were subordinated. Sustained, though, why? Because you have not taken care, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, to ensure that they are properly and fully served. You came and legislated organic positions in 2018 and until we received in July 2019 there was no ASP notice coming out and we got it through direct and rapid procedures. We therefore set limits on the mobility of civil servants from the welfare centres who, once they came to the centres after a while, left for other services. Then, in this bill, we adopt the tax-free and inappropriate annual income support for the permanent inhabitants of mountainous and disadvantaged regions, an allowance which they have received so far but which counts for the annual taxable income. We exempt this draft law from any netting of public debts, other public sector bodies and credit institutions, so that as a genuine social policy measure we can guarantee the income of the beneficiary families and provide for the non-assessment of the aid to the income ceilings for the payment of any other provision of a social or prosperous nature. Ladies and gentlemen, we expressly set out in this bill on the last working day of each month as the day of payment of allowances, because payment of all allowances is unreasonable in the way you have left it to be, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA. You've been hampering the beneficiaries of benefits for a long time. You put them in front of television receivers on the morning news show waiting for the bar to pass, to inform them when you will decide to give them each allowance. Fiesta, communication! The beneficiaries, however, do not see them as a show but as an aid to organise their family financial planning. Social policy does not go through press releases, where the date of payment of benefits is indicated. Social policy is in practice evidenced by those who, in fact, need state support. Many allowances granted by the agency do not have a certain payment date. That is why we say that all benefits will be granted on the last working day of the month and finally. We will not do the "Agiosilis" whenever it suits us for voting purposes like you. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, we provide the opportunity to participate in the municipality funding programme. Unfortunately, there are municipalities in the country where infants, children or nurseries do not operate and which, however, have properties which can accommodate such structures. This regulation enables municipalities which have structures and building facilities to be able to join the programme that they could not before. The possibility of financing these is to establish new sections of infant, child and baby care, and to cover the need to find jobs and municipalities or legal persons who do not have such structures. The main pillar of our social policy is the care for the child and the effort to improve demographic indicators in the country. That is why we are aiming not only at the growth of existing structures but also at the creation of further facilities and accommodation places, structures and positions which will be made available for pre-school children by public bodies. Municipalities operating up to two parts of infant, child or baby care may participate in the financing programme for the operation as four sections and municipalities that had ready-to-do buildings and did not enter the programme can now set up up up to two infant, child or nursery stations. No children, ladies and gentlemen, no children, no children's station! You tabled an amendment, ladies and gentlemen of the SYRIZA, for the permanentisation of CSR officials and because the amendment signs it all the leadership of the Ministry of Home Affairs, Labour and Social Solidarity in the Government of SYRIZA, is reasonably created both to me and to other colleagues but above all to the Greek people the following question: You were a government, Mrs Fotou, more than four years ago. What have you done to date on this matter? And yet another technical and substantial aspect, that the staffing of the JAPDs is the responsibility of the Ministry of the Interior and Economics. However, the issue of CSR and CSR - AIA concerns us and we are particularly sensitive to this. For this reason, the Ministry will examine the framework for the operation and authorisation of the JAPDs. Subsequently, we redefined the way in which dues from employers' contributions were settled until 31/12-2018 to the social security institutions and specified the terms and conditions under which the central office of the Centre for the Collection of Insurance Debts shall delete dues dues for legal or natural persons until 31 January of the following calendar year. We are derecognising dues of twenty-year-old debts created until 31 January 2021 up to EUR 200 per debtor and outstanding accredited debts per year up to EUR 10 per debtor. With the implementation of these write-offs, we prevent the accumulation of small debts of doubtful collectivity, which puts brakes on strategic planning. We are thus helping to improve the functioning of the body. We reduce the capital for issuing notifications and we release staff from seeking to collect those debts. In many cases the amount due is less than its collection costs, resulting in any procedure for this purpose causing a disproportionate administrative burden. We provide additional support in the context of the digital transformation of the EBRD in the year 2020 to enable the development of e-government-level level 4 transactions and the electronic transaction of the insured with the services of the ECA. We facilitate citizens by carrying out the relevant electronic procedures. Without a compulsory visit to the OLAF branches, we are saving working hours and we are not afflicting the citizen. Ladies and gentlemen, we have said so at committee meetings, but I mention it here too. It was the government of New Democracy, which first established in Greece the minimum guaranteed income under Minister Giannis Vroutzis. The minimum guaranteed income was placed in a pilot scheme entitled ‘Pilot programme of minimum guaranteed income’ under Law 4093/2012. SYRIZA simply changed the name to one of the many baptisms he made during his term of office. You have baptized the minimum guaranteed income in CRLs. We do not, however, live in SYRIZA's micropsy, but we must also be in politics with great vigour. We don't care about rhetoric and baptism. We are interested in producing real work, delivering a state to the citizens better financially and organisationally than we have received. We now have the minimum guaranteed income to the Prime Minister of Kyriakos Mitsotakis and are constitutionally guaranteed under Article 21 of the Constitution. It does not depend on the will of each government and every Minister. It is a constitutional provision, because this is what we proposed and voted by an absolute majority during the last Greek Review House, a measure of paramount importance which is a manifestation of social welfare, respect for the citizen and the social rule of law. Ladies and gentlemen, this draft law is a social bill with implications of both national and insurance. The Government and the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs have once again moved in the right direction. We are doing targeted and responsible for solving the problems, always having a basic pillar of our society's political demands and human value. In expressing the pulse of society, we are bringing this bill to a vote, a bill which is part of the government's overall effort to resolve the major problem of demographics. They establish a demographic policy with the grant of the birth allowance, an effort that we have so far framed outside of the birth allowance and day-care facilities and other measures such as the reduction of VAT on infants to 13% from 24%, the abolition of luxury living tax on private vehicles of more than 2 000 cubic metres, the strengthening of duty-free 1 000 euros for each minor child and others, which clearly shows the axis of the Government of Kyriakos Mitsotakis, an anthropocentric axis. So, today, we are establishing a modern demographic policy with the grant of the birth grant, we are founding the future of Greece, a future based on the economic sustainable development of the country, the reversal of the climate of ingenuity and the restoration of trust between the citizen and the state that has been so missing in recent years. With this draft law, we are setting a good start for the country's general demographic policy, a policy aimed at increasing the incentives for births of more children in order to reverse the climate created by the demographic collapse of our country. And other initiatives will be taken on the part of the government. As yet a bill by the Government of New Democracy and Mr Mitsotakis we are doing nothing more than self-evident, that is, what is in need of immediate regulation and which we have identified as bad texts from the previous government. We do not make cheap donation policies and phantoms. Here, moreover, there is a huge ideological difference between us and the obsessive and ideoleptic SYRIZA. Our policy is development. We always believe in work on equal opportunities and always supporting our most vulnerable fellow citizens. It's our great ideological difference. We have a realistic policy to implement what we have announced. And most importantly we bring practical bills, bills that will make their results felt to the entire Greek people from the next day. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u20	It is now called the Special Rapporteur by Mera25 Mr. Apatzides Maria.
ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u21	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, Mr Ministers, as a PART25, we are, in principle, critical of the spirit of the bill. The core of the bill is the 2,000 euro allowance for every child born in our country. This, in the expressions used in the bill, is a move that aspires to be one of the new foundations of the country's modern demographic policy, the structured development of which must be a constant priority of the state. It is expected to be an incentive for future parents and, at the same time, a significant aid in the first upbringing period of their child. We are, in principle, opposed to the logic of the bill, for two different reasons, a theoretical and practical one. Firstly, at the theoretical level, the spirit of the bill is one of what a left-wing view can be called 'biopolitical', i.e. political definition of life, focusing on the state's planning of births through incentives or compulsions. Biopolitics is about management and regulation techniques of life and the death of human populations by power. It means that politics has the potential to shape the life of the human species, according to its purposes, in order to consolidate and increase its power. Thus, the birth rate of the citizens of a nation of a state is included in the demographic issue as a state-of-the-art issue and the political power is called upon to regulate the extent to which citizens give birth or do not give birth by political and government means. Today we call them motives. At Mer25, we think it is not the State's job to force a woman directly or indirectly to childbirth. It is a state's job, however, if a woman has already felt the desire and made the decision to give birth, then to provide her with a good environment for childbirth, childbirth, development and blooming. For this reason, as a PART25, we are in favour of helping the state create a wider environment, which helps the development of the family, in line with the Constitution, as well as with the wider political culture. But we're against biopolitical measures in the narrow sense. In any case, we propose the political culture and the social body that it creates, above all, fears that their tension leads to the search for biological solutions. I'll explain more specifically what I mean. Usually, there are factors more important than the direct biopolitical logic of providing incentives. Often, they fall into the function of whether or not a woman decides to give birth to a variety of economic factors, from her income to the actual cost of childbirth. These real obstacles to implementing a woman’s will for childbearing or family, obviously need to concern us. This is the second reason why we are in principle opposed to the bill. This is that even practically, the predictions of the bill are in the wrong direction. No single gift of EUR 2 000 can solve the very real economic problems faced by future mothers. If anyone wanted to solve the financial obstacles that future or new mothers find before them, there are provisions that could make a real difference. I shall mention some indicative but important proposals. It takes the creation of state obstetrics to transfer the cost of birth from each family to the whole of society, methods of assisted fertilization must be subsidised by the insurance funds, there must be a gradual reduction in parental insurance contributions, in addition to the two children, which is essential and totally different from what the government has announced. State aid must be provided in order to eliminate, with the care of the state, the costs that the employer is currently paying for the birth of children by employees in the private sector. This leads today to unacceptable pressures on private sector employees, which have the effect of banning childbirth. It also needs a long-term policy on the welfare of children, including the introduction of universal, free care and quality pre-school education. This would help parents continue to work at the same time, to care for their children and also to provide free medical, ophthalmic and dental care for every child, from birth to eighteen years, as well as a full and free annual preventive medical examination, from birth until the age of 12. The application of such solutions would help women to give birth to and raise their children with confidence. It would indeed be a observance of both the Greek constitution and European political culture. I now look at these articles. Articles 1 to 12 are a first part relating to the birth allowance. In principle, we are critical. Of course, we will not vote against an allowance that might be some kind of help. We note, however, that it is wrongly targeted. Article 1 "By birth allowance" establishes a birth allowance of EUR 2,000 for each child born in Greece from 1-1-2020. We shall vote'residents' for the reasons I have just mentioned. Similarly, we will vote'residences' in Articles 2 to 6 and in Articles 8 and 11 on procedural matters relating to the grant of the allowance. I will only dwell on Article 2, 'on the basis of the equivalent family income', which provides that the birth allowance will be paid in two equal instalments. This creates a problem for parents, who will have to pay instantly and one-off for their maternity. It would have been more practical, the allowance would have been given at first, so that the motherhood and parents would not be found in despair. At the same time, Article 2 provides for the possibility to submit the relevant application either to the civil registry information system subsystem for the birth declaration or to the OPEKA system directly for the birth allowance, always via an electronic application. We welcome this automation, but this does not change our general reluctance. The article, in which we are most critical, is Article 7 ‘categories of beneficiaries’. Article 7 introduces a criterion of legal and permanent residence in the Greek territory of the beneficiaries of the birth allowance. For citizens of third States who do not fall under the regulatory regime in cases (b) to (d) of the proposed provision, a 12-year stay is required in the country before the application for the birth allowance is made. As a MERA25, we are many critics of this regulation, which essentially states a fearlessness, contrary to European political culture. Furthermore, Article 9 provides that the birth allowance is to be granted by the Social Solidarity Beneficiary - OPEKA - to which we are in agreement. Also, under Article 10, which provides that the birth allowance does not fall under any category of income, so that it is exempted from any tax, levy, levy or detention in favour of the public or third parties, and we are surely in agreement. Similarly, Article 12 provides for the inclusion of appropriations for the implementation of the birth allowance in the budget of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs, which has the same purpose: it grants the Social Solidarity Beneficiaries Agency. This is a right move, with which we agree. Article 13 creates a birth subsystem in the civil register. In this way, the birth process is simplified and automated. This is a necessary development, with which we agree. Similarly, Article 14 adds the number of the Tax Register and the Child’s Social Security Register to the information to be included in the birth permit, with a view to introducing to the public administration the new automated procedure for the issue of VAT and AMCA to citizens born in Greece and, in accordance with the applicable legislation, they are entitled to them. The new procedure will relieve public administration of bureaucracy, citizens of additional burdens and limit the risk of incorrect registrations in the respective registers. And with that, we certainly agree. So far, I have mentioned, on the one hand, our criticism in the first 14 articles and, on the other, certain specific articles, with which we agree that they do serve the citizen. I now come to our main criticism, which concerns some of the other provisions. Our main criticism lies in the fact that we consider that this allowance to be essentially fenades to cover its dreadful choices so that thousands of third-country citizens lose the three basic allowances: the child's allowance, the housing allowance and the former social solidarity income - CRL - which renames a minimum guaranteed income. It is precisely these cuts that are hidden in the 'other provisions'. This is done through two filters: firstly, the abolition of allowances, even if one of the children of the family repeats the order because of absence, secondly, the reduction of allowances, depending on the origin of the children, since the criterion of their parents' permanent and legal residence is not dealt with uniformly. These are choices, which are contrary to many international and European rules of law, but also to provisions of the Greek Constitution. Let us see in more detail: in Article 15, citizens of third countries who have been recognised as beneficiaries of subsidiary protection in compliance with international rules of increased standard law, as well as primary and secondary rules of European Union law, as these rules have been incorporated into Greek law. At the same time, differences in the duration of the criterion of residence to the citizens of third countries are preferable. It provides for the 12 years, up to the period of residence of these citizens of third countries. It should be noted here that there were five years before. In particular, for the purpose of granting the allowance, it is made as a precondition not only to register the child at school, but also to receive his actual student, in such a way that they are excluded from the grant of the allowance in the case of children who are forced to repeat the same order because of the large number of their absences from school. This provision aims to eliminate the phenomenon of the abstention of minors from their school duties and hence to strengthen the compulsory education system by introducing an incentive effect for parents to take care of them as beneficiaries of the allowance. We regard these wording as hypocritical. The Government is attempting to minimize the number of beneficiaries and hypocritically surrounds its choice with supposedly loving interest in school abstention, while at the same time making it responsible for losing the child's allowance. This is an article in which we are extremely critical. In Article 16, the Government appears - supposedly - to make it possible for beneficiaries to receive the allowance until the provision of Article 15 has been introduced. We regard this as hypocritical. In Article 17, we have a similar hypocrisy to Article 15. In addition, this is where the wicked wording is introduced to provide adequate care for every childless member of the household. This means that if even one of the family’s children repeats order, due to school absences, then the family is not entitled to the allowance. This is a provision that aims to cut many of the beneficiaries off, and that is why we are extremely critical. In Article 18 again, the government appears - supposedly - to do the favor of the already beneficiaries, in a hypocritical manner. Article 19 ‘Transition or secondment of employees from the Social Welfare Centres’ we consider it to include provisions of a clipboard nature, which are not sufficient. Article 20 contains provisions to support low-income families living in mountain and disadvantaged areas by paying annual income support. This is indeed a positive measure that we are in agreement with. On the contrary, Article 21 is very much of a concern to us. Under the pretext of speed, promptness and flexibility, the current legislative framework on the number and properties of members of the Board of Directors of the National Centre for Social Solidarity - ECA - is amended - and the present Board of Directors ceases. Unfortunately, on the basis of all the samples that the government has given up to now, we fear that this is another attempt to cover the positions of the board of directors from the governing party. Article 22 agrees with us, because the setting of a specific day for the provision of services by the Social Solidarity Fund - OPEKA - is indeed likely to resolve a number of problems that have so far been identified. Article 23 provides for the possibility for municipalities where no infant, child or nursing establishments are working to request funding if they have real estate in which such structures can be hosted. However, it is not clear whether and how to control the use of these funds by these municipalities. We are sceptical, and we think there should be a greater statement in this regard. With Article 24, we do not disagree. We believe that what it reveals is the uncertainty of both the previous and the current government. We agree with Article 25. Article 26 states that the debtors covered by this arrangement are to be granted a discount on all kinds of indiscriminate increases of status, employer and size of an undertaking. We are not opposed to this regulation in particular, but we are critical of the overall context, as I have already said. With Article 27 on deletion of unsuccessful debts for very small amounts, we agree. We also agree with Article 28 on e-commerce of insured persons, because the possibility of e-government transactions at level 4 is introduced in the context of the digital transformation of the EBRD. The citizens are thus allowed to complete the relevant electronically without a visit to the branch of the ECA. Finally, we are very critical of Article 29 for the minimum guaranteed income. This provides that the grant of the allowance is conditional, not only on the registration of the child at school, but also on his actual attendance. However, they are excluded from the grant of the household allowance, which their minors are obliged to repeat the same order because of the large number of absences from school. This provision is supposed to eliminate the phenomenon of the abstention of minors from their school duties and thus strengthen the compulsory education system. In fact, however, beneficiaries are being cut off in a way that shows unjustified fearfulness and punishment. That's what we disagree with. I conclude here the detailed position of Mera25, summarizing that in the Mera25 our priority is to formulate the best possible environment, so that a woman can bring her child to the world. We are vigilant about the messages that sends a once-incentive adjustment to dying in a violent political way in a certain direction. Most of all, however, we are critical of the combination of the fearfulness and hypocrisy of the government, which, as we have tried to show, is contrary to European political culture. We are voting on the principle of 'the present'. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u31	Ladies and gentlemen, with the draft legislation being passed, the Government is implementing a basic electoral commitment by the New Democracy and at the same time, it is institutionally putting in place a systematic effort to deal with the demographic crisis in our country. A crisis that, if we do not take action today, will threaten the country's existence for decades that come, both at socio-economic and geopolitical level. It is obvious that reversal of a trend towards demographics is a complex matter and any initiatives that will give rise in time. This does not mean, however, that we should underestimate the positive effect that may have very targeted actions and targeted measures in this direction, as well as their impact on collective psychology, but also on citizens' personal weightings, with regard to the decision to take a position of responsibility and to start with a new family. Ladies and gentlemen, in addition to the grant of the birth allowance, this bill regulates, in particular, issues of preferential benefits, which are already granted, in order to increase their positive impact on both the beneficiaries and the social body in general. That's why we need all of you all to do this. We want you allies, not only in the first part of the bill, that with the high budget expenditure of 173 million euros, which is intended to help our demographic problem, but also in the substantial reforms that we are doing and to help the effectiveness of our country's welfare and benefits policy. I now come to the essence of the provisions, a birth allowance. ELSTAT's latest statistics show us that births last year fell by 2.3%, and deaths also fell by 3.4%. It is also clear from this that the decline in birth in our country has been lower than the corresponding average in the European Union over the last decade. This, coupled with the fact that more and more of us decide to have children at a higher age means that families eventually have a rate of growth of less than two. So the country's population is constantly shrinking. These tendencies are obviously not only recorded in Greece, they are many other European Union countries which have negative demographic trends, but the economic crisis that our country has experienced over the past decade has led to the difficulty of obtaining the necessary conditions for their families and children. For this reason, the introduction of a birth allowance is a necessity, following the practice of other European countries such as the United Kingdom, Switzerland, Luxembourg and Belgium. Demographic is a national issue, it is a development issue and it is a matter of insurance. The decline in the birth rate is obviously also linked to the prospect of our insurance system. Because the decline in births and the ageing of the working population means that the labour force is falling and the pensioners in our country are growing. These are two parallel trends that threaten the sustainability of the social security system. In these circumstances a modern demographic policy is more than necessary. It's urgent. The introduction for the first time of the birth allowance is a crucial component, essentially, but also symbolically, of such a policy. The allowance which will be inconsistent, I stress, will have a horizontal and almost universal character, which is why it is subject to two basic conditions. Firstly, a very high and second income limit of permanent and legal residence in our country. According to the Economic Experts' Council, the income ceiling that we set as a limit - that of a family to have a child, its first child, EUR 70,000, thus an equivalent income of EUR 40,000 - is only about 1% of the most prosperous Greeks. The allowance will be granted to Greeks who stay permanently in our country, will be granted to homosexuals, citizens of the European Union and will also be granted to citizens of the EEA, but also to citizens of Switzerland, as well as to citizens of third countries. But citizens from third countries who have lived permanently and legally in our country for over twelve years. This is because twelve years we think it is a year that is capable of ensuring a living bond between the beneficiary and the Greek society. The provision for a 12-year stay is linked to the demographic objective of the measure, as it does not mean granting such financial support to people for whom their long-term establishment is not guaranteed in the country and therefore their integration into the Greek society is not guaranteed. I note here that the birth allowance will be the first allowance to be granted fully automatedly, and that is why we are very happy with our cooperation with the Ministry of Digital Governance. And of course, as has already been pointed out, the birth allowance is merely one of the measures of an integrated demographic policy, which we have already established. I would remind the delegation that our Government has already legislated and implemented many other measures to reverse the demographic trend, such as EUR 1,000 tax free for every child, a reduction in the value added tax rate from 24% to 13% for infants and child seats, and the abolition of luxury tax on vehicles over two thousand cubic cubic families. After all, it was also within the proposals of the Intergroup on Population of the Parliament of 2018. It is of crucial importance for us, and perhaps this, if you like, is also the front of an integrated demographic policy. It is the introduction in 2020 of an extended system of access to nurseries and this through a global financial support, a medium-sized EUR 180 for all families and beneficiaries. In order to fulfil this objective, however, we also act within this draft law. How do we do that? Within Article 21, access by municipalities is currently facilitated by funding which already exists but has not been absorbed in order to establish and operate more kindergartens. The second part of the bill is about updating the conditions of use of four other basic benefits. In particular, the minimum guaranteed income, the child benefit, the housing allowance and the mountainous disadvantaged areas. There, ladies and gentlemen, we would like to see you allies, because it is not enough to vote only on this part of what gives benefits. It is essential that we also vote for fundamental reforms. And for us, the linking of subsidised policy, by ensuring that our children go to school, is absolutely a priority. And I stress 'they have been staying legally and permanently for over twelve years'. I would point out, however, that a condition for the 12-year stay does not concern these applications and that it does not concern the existing beneficiaries in order not to disrupt the economic planning of thousands of households in our country. In addition - and there we want allies and wonder about the absence of this alliance - this bill provides that benefits will henceforth be paid for a single day. All allowances from the OPECA will be paid in the last working month. This is very important to us. It is important that our most vulnerable fellow citizens, who are beneficiaries of the allowances, can receive allowances on the same day, on the last day of the month, so that they know in advance how to organise their family budget. Unfortunately, however, during the debate on the bill in the committee responsible, the Qualified Opposition cannot deny the importance of the birth allowance has once again produced reports of poor quality opposition. Mrs Fotou herself, the former Minister, reportedly reported cuts hidden in the second part of the bill, to the minimum guaranteed income, which SYRIZA allegedly introduced for the first time. This is double inaccurate. First of all, because this allowance had been introduced for the first time by the Government of New Democracy and, secondly, because there was absolutely no reduction as confirmed by the report of the General Accounting Office of the State, neither the per capita aid or the total number of beneficiaries of the minimum guaranteed income. Ladies and gentlemen, finally it seems that SYRIZA misunderstands the quality of the Mitsotakis Government and perceives it as an opportunity to reverse reality. But citizens cannot be confused and forget the truth, and I explain it. We have criticised the fact that the birth allowance will be given in two instalments, which Mrs Fotio also said disturbs family planning. Of course, he misleaded that this programming was being assisted since such an allowance was not given. However, we have not heard anything about the positive measure of paying all these allowances, which are included in this bill, on the last working day of each month and not depending on the availability of each minister who may have given two months together as a bonus and then nothing for a long time. People are aware of these truths and do not forget them. Nor can they forget that SYRIZA was the one who disappeared by the EVS and the current New Democracy government is the one trying to make up for the losses of the low-pensions from the abolition of this allowance, with measures such as the permanent aid mechanism and yesterday's amendment for the full exemption of the low-pensions from their involvement in medicines. So we are doing - as with the new insurance which will soon come to Parliament - an appeal to the impact of the Katorugalos Act, one of which was, unfortunately, the abolition of the CSCE. This is, unfortunately, for SYRIZA, the truth and thankfully the citizens know it. Madam Minister, this bill actually goes beyond the granting of a single 2 000 euro grant for every new child. It attempts to tackle the demographic problem with the overall re-introduction of family welfare policy through the granting of financial aid, such as birth allowance, child benefit, housing allowance and minimum guaranteed income, which, after the last constitutional review introduced by the New Democracy, is now protected by Article 21 of the Constitution, and - I remind you - in conjunction with the already voted-in reduction in VAT for infants, the EUR 1,000 tax free for every child and measures for multiples. Also, with regard to the allowance for mountain and less-favoured areas, the bill establishes the tax-free and irreparable amount of aid. This takes into account the additional living costs borne by families, which are unfavourable because of their geographical isolation. Apart from an active public policy providing financial incentives for new births, however, the daily facilitation of the family and especially of the working mother is also needed, for example, by means of more nursery facilities. That is why this bill also facilitates access by all municipalities to public funding for the purpose of operating more kindergartens in order to ensure a position for all families in need. At the same time, we are trying to involve the downward course of the active population with the so-called Brain Drain phenomenon. For the latter, the same Minister for Labour, Mr Brutsis, has promised that soon the program will be launched to attract Greek scientists abroad to repatriate, with a view to their employment in sectors of the private sector. It is a measure that will not only provide necessary know-how in many productive sectors, but we hope that it will also stop the wave of fleeing and other young people who have to give much to Greek society. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to conclude by congratulating the Ministry of Labour's leadership on another central commitment to start the electronic insurance issue in five months' time. With the planned integration of the funds and with all the insurance data in common, the new body, e-ECA, will be able to issue at the same time a master, subsidiary and one in three pending cases at the same time. This is the top initiative of the Ministry of Labour that comes to button with digitisation of insurance data and certificates that are under way. These important interventions are designed by the Ministry of Labour without drum impacts and bakery. We leave others the alteration of reality, the denial of the progress of the Mitsotakis Government and the extremist criticism of everything. This is because in the New Democracy we believe that only by stable, serious and creative steps can we go ahead with the social state. That is why we are voting in favour of this bill, supporting the government and the upcoming major battle for the new insurance policy. Thank you.

As long as you see relevant studies and as long as it starts from the empirical given that for the first time post-war, the population has fallen from 11 million in the 2011 census, to 10 million seventeen thousand today. There are many studies - I have heard Mr Loverdos mentioning some elements - which say that in 2050 there is a prospect of having fallen even below nine million. Demographic is, if you want, the number one national theme and we have to do everything we can to manage this course, to go to the best of the scenarios there are. Politicians such as those provided for in this draft law are absolutely in the right direction and act beneficially. I would like to mention a little more in detail in Article 13 of this bill, in relation to the automation of the child's birth declaration to maternity care, which is part of a wider strategy, that of building a digital state that is simple, fast and effective and thus establishing this new process. I do not think we need to say a lot about the existing procedure. Whoever we're parents, we've experienced her. To date, a child, when born, needs to gather a number of documents, you need to first go to the JEPs, then to the maternity office itself to make a birth certificate, one needs to go to the registry office to write the birth permit in ten days, and then there is a need to make the birth statement to other institutions, for example in the calendar - that is now done automatically, it has not been done before -, AMCA, the father or mother's insurer, and of course also to the providers of benefits. Of all the above stages, all that has been removed, as I mentioned, was the statement in the land register. All the rest is a series of bureaucratic steps which, to date, need to be implemented in such a type of process. Multiple multiple-step queues, very often not even the services in the same municipality. What are we doing today? The birth declaration process begins and ends in the maternity ward. More specifically since the middle of the month, since the end of February, parents will come to the hospital service and after their identification all the supporting documents will be automatically collected from the civil registry or automatically sought electronically. In this way, a birth declaration will be issued, which will be sent by electronic means to the competent registry as pending and the registry will in turn issue, on the basis of these data, the birth permit, which will again be sent to a number of entities, such as in the AMKA register, the AMK register, the bridal register and thus effectively process the AMKA return, the registration in the family portion and will be updated by third registries, such as the AMKA for the fund. This whole process will be handled without the need for actions by the parents, who will be automatically informed either by sms or by e-mail regarding the evolution of the process, with the performance of AMKA, by registering the insurance fund. Of course, if there are any loose ends, they will also be informed of them. There is also an additional stage, which will also be handled electronically. It is also the most correlated with the adoption of the new procedure with the draft law to be passed, the automatic electronic performance of the birth allowance. And this will be done as follows: OPECA will draw the financial data from the parents' ADI in order to verify that the criteria we have set in total are met, as to whether a couple can get a specific allowance or a parent. That is how we create an additional service without adding another line for the citizens. Also, here the update will be made through sms or e-mail. This will gradually be done for most citizens' interactions with the state through the use of digital technologies we will simplify as much procedures as possible. It is the so-called 'life events' and it is perhaps symbolic that we are starting today at birth. Let me close with this thought: one of the main election demands has been codified in the following sentence: 'Build a state'. State of course we do. What we need to do is bring it to 2020. And that's what we're doing. Actions such as this are an important step forward, whether we are talking about digitising the birth declaration, whether we are talking about simplifying the renewal of a driving licence, whether we are talking about a responsible statement, an authorisation, the electronic prescription to be made intact. These are all steps forward. Perhaps on a symbolic level, as I said before, there was no better way to start from this process, the process of a child's birth. Thank you very much.

The allowance is granted even subject to the permanent and legal residence in the country and provided that the applicant or applicant is a Greek citizen, a resident or foreigner, a citizen of a Member State of the European Union, a citizen of a State belonging to the European Economic Area or a citizen of the Swiss Confederation or, finally, a third-country citizen who has already resided 12 years in our country. The allowance will be paid in two instalments of equal amounts, following the submission of a relevant electronic application or digital declaration to the maternity hospital, i.e. the usual bureaucracy in the granting of benefits to date. Particularly important is that this allowance will not be taxed or subject to fees, contributions or any other form of booking. Also, it will not be seized, it will not be offset against any debt. It is an allowance, therefore, that is intended to help the family of the newborn and we must ensure that no one is deprived of this aid for the wrong reasons. Ladies and gentlemen, it is certainly not panacea for the problems of demographics, but it is a first, important step in the fight against fertility, a measure which is part of a broader policy of strengthening the Greek family and ensuring a more prosperous future for children in our country. In this context, it is, inter alia, important to facilitate access by municipalities which do not have nurseries to the necessary funding and to link grants to adequate school provision for children. Thank you very much for your time.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u99	Thank you, Madam President. Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, we are today called upon to vote in favour of a bill of paramount importance, because it concerns the future and the existence of the Greek nation. Already since the late 1980s, it has been found that in Greece the modern fertility index has passed its critical level of one and a half children per woman, thus marking the passage to a phase that experts call "low fertility trap", with a significant risk that it will not be able to return above this level for a long time. With the manifestation of the economic crisis, which has been so brutally experienced by our country, which has also brought about a social crisis, there is a steady decline in births. The economic downturn, which is usually associated with job instability, with increased uncertainty and a reduction in real incomes, directly affects family income, which is inextricably related to childcare and is therefore likely to cause significant deferral and reduction in births. Fear of low income, coupled with a large proportion of jobseekers, leads to a postponement of births. Demography is a decisive factor in our future, which we no longer have the luxury to underestimate. The reduction in population in itself is not the only demographic challenge we have to face. It is not only the size of our population that is changing, it changes its structure and composition, since births are less than deaths, so that we have an ageing population, with the known consequences for the quality and quantity of the workforce, the reduction of the competitive power of the working population, the burden of the insurance system and the collapse of our pension system. Undoubtedly, demographic is today one of the great challenges facing the country and very high on the New Democracy agenda. Enhancing births and supporting the family was, is, is and will be our primary goal. This is why a coherent demographic policy is necessary. This draft law is now in progress. The measures introduced aim to reverse the negative phenomenon of ingenuity and to form a modern and targeted demographic policy, which will encourage couples to proceed with childbirth. Economic uncertainty is an inhibiting factor in childbirth. The aim of the 2,000 euro birth allowance, as well as of the other benefits provided for in this bill, i.e. children, housing, minimum guaranteed income and benefit of mountainous disadvantaged areas, is to create a sense of economic stability and security for couples who want to have children. It is very important to stress that there are almost no income criteria for the beneficiaries of the birth allowance, that this is not taxed or subject to reservations, it is not confiscated. Equally important, if not the most important, is the linking of benefits to school children, because at our centre is and should be the child. It is our duty to ensure that each child receives the education in which he has a constitutional right. Education is the most important tool for every citizen, because it is a basic prerequisite for his social, economic and cultural development, but also for our country. The measures at present are not detached, but they are part of a comprehensive policy of support for the family, the couple, the woman who wants to give birth. We have already legislated to reduce VAT on baby products, but above all we have guaranteed accessibility to childcare structures. In particular, the second is crucial for women and men to be able, in other words, to take care of the child, to participate in the labour market. The main aim of our policy is to facilitate the combination of professional and family life, taking into account all lifestyles, as they form within our society. The aim is to balance between working and family life, so as not to prevent the participation of a mother in the labour market and to encourage her development in the labour market. We do not in any way want to reproduce the model of the non-working mother. Moreover, given the reduction in the available workforce, increasing the particularly low employment rates of women is one of the tools to address the consequences of ageing. Women's employment is not negatively related to fertility. Finland, Sweden and Norway are the countries with the lowest difference in employment rates between men and women and at the same time the highest levels of fertility. The income of women is now positively linked to fertility, which has also been confirmed by Greek data over the last decade. During the crisis women who increased their fertility were the working women over thirty-five years old. We want the woman who wants to become a mother not to be discouraged because of economic insecurity or to hesitate to fear that childcare will cut her off from the labour market. As a woman I am proud of this bill and also of the overall plan to support motherhood and woman, prepared by our Government and implements a step forward, because this is a package that aims to remove the obstacles that the woman who wants to become a mother finds herself. It's a bill both substantial and symbolic. Of course, financial support is important. But what excitements me is symbolism. We support the mother, the woman, the worker. We want the Greek woman to have the opportunity to achieve everything she dreams about: motherhood, family and professional recognition. The least we can do today, ladies and gentlemen, is to give the Greek mother a vote of confidence. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u118	Thank you, too. Mr Leonardis has the floor now from New Democracy. To prepare Mr. Kotsiras also from the New Democracy.
ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u119	Ladies and gentlemen, this bill entitled 'A birth allowance and other provisions' of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs is a first response to a chronic and two-fold problem affecting the Greek state, that of demographic ageing and subnatality. In particular, during the years of the economic crisis which hit our country, many families have either postponed their having children indefinitely or were confined to one child. The reason is that they were unable to offer the conditions consistent with a decent standard of living. The population contraction is not an exclusive feature of Greece, but of other more advanced European countries. However, the constantly deteriorating demographic problem takes comparatively greater proportions in our country, which is still growing further with inertia to take concrete measures to strengthen it. However, with the current government, Greece is turning a page firmly with the introduction of a birth allowance of 2,000 euros for every child born in Greece from 1-1-2020. This allowance will be paid in two equal instalments of EUR 1,000. As is clear from the title of the bill, it is a pre-innovation allowance covering the widest range of social groups and, above all, most patients. That is why it is irreversible, untaxable and aims to be a substantial incentive for future parents to acquire offspring and, of course, an important aid to the early and increased costs of raising the child. A criterion of legal and permanent residence in the Greek territory of the beneficiaries of the birth allowance is also introduced in order to have a strong link between the above beneficiaries and the host State. For citizens of third States not covered by the regulatory regime in specific cases, a 12-year stay shall be required in the country before the application for the birth allowance is lodged. The birth allowance is granted on the basis of the equivalent family income, which is set at EUR 40,000 per annum, as shown by the tax declaration and the related administrative determination of tax. As in every policy area, in this case of the birth grant it must be clearly and above all rational criteria for granting it, which will take account of the country's financial possibilities, respect its status and be in line with the general feeling of law. So that is what we are doing today. Among other positive arrangements in this bill is that an application for a birth allowance is made electronically either in the subsystem of the civil registry information system for the birth declaration or directly in the OPEKA system. In this way we support digital governance and the gradual digital transformation of the country, by achieving a reduction in bureaucratic burdens and by avoiding errors affecting both public services and citizens. Our aim is for the citizen to feel that the state is standing next to its needs in the most modern way, which cannot be electronic interaction and service. Apart from the birth allowance, however, we have sought to reorientate the map and other critical benefits, such as the housing allowance, the child's allowance, the benefit of mountainous and disadvantaged areas and the minimum guaranteed income. For the purpose of granting housing allowances, a child and a minimum guaranteed income is defined as a prerequisite for a documented adequate school for minors and not just for their registration at school. This option is not punishable and is not intended to cut benefits. The aim is to eliminate the phenomenon of unjustified abstention of minors from the school environment and complete compulsory education. Parents are also required to ensure that their children are treated uninterruptedly in order to obtain state protection. For us education is a valuable good that no child should be deprived of. Instead, the acquisition of knowledge is at the forefront of our policy, so that every child can be able to raise socially, personally and self-regulatoryly. At the same time, an allowance for mountainous and disadvantaged areas is continued and granted. This allowance aims to strengthen citizens who live permanently in areas of the country with particularly geographical and economic characteristics. However, the tax-free and incompatibility of the household allowance for mountain and disadvantaged areas with low incomes is being introduced. This aid is exempted from the possibility of offsetting it with accredited public debts, other public sector bodies and credit institutions. Another innovation of the bill is the emphasis given to the need to increase the care structures of infants and infants. This provides the opportunity to fund through the program managed by the Hellenic Society of Local Development and Administration of the municipalities and legal persons in which no childcare and childcare departments operate. In Greece only 8.9% of children under three years of age have access to care infrastructure, far away from the European Union's target, 33% for 2020. In our opinion, care for children of pre-school age is a key pillar of childcare and of the wider project to improve the country's demographic indicators. Our government's priority is to broaden the existing structures, but also to create new facilities and accommodations by public institutions for pre-school children. This will support the foundation of the family and some parents may be able to reintegrate into the labour market after they have been unloaded on the day-to-day of their children. Ladies and gentlemen, we are listening to the pulse of society and setting a new foundation for a modern demographic policy in the country. Clearly, what we are legislating today is not enough in a period of depopulation of the countryside and ageing population that tends to take dangerous national dimensions. We also need a set of measures and incentives to enable further strengthening of the country's demographic policy by strengthening family and social cohesion. And I believe that the government and Prime Minister of the country Kyriakos Mitsotakis is moving in this direction - step by step: the duty-freeness of EUR 1,000 for every child, a reduction in VAT on the purchase of infants at 13%, an allowance of EUR 180, so that every child can be at a nursery station and now a birth allowance of EUR 2 000. I must congratulate the leadership of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs and our Prime Minister, because this bill I believe is a step that can help families to have even more children. It is a first step and I believe that other measures will be taken in this direction. Thank you.

The proportion of the elderly in the population as a whole will increase significantly over the next few decades, as a significant proportion of the generation of the post-war birth explosion, the so-called "baby boom", reaches the retirement limit. This, in turn, will lead to an increased burden on people who are working age, who should in turn bear the social costs, which are required by the ageing population for a number of relevant policies and services. In the face of this reality and all these samples, therefore, the current government establishes an allowance of 1-1-2020 for the birth of each child, almost without income criteria. Greece, even today, is finding it difficult to redefine the conditions for healthy development and resolution of demographics. We are proposing the establishment of the Special Secretariat for Demographic Policy, the drawing up of a national plan for demographic demography by a newly established National Demographic Policy Committee, the establishment of effective advisory and support centres for the municipalities, the subsidisation of subgenous couples for the purposes of assisted reproduction, the transport - and this is very important - of basic baby species in the excessively reduced VAT rate, the safeguarding clause for the rights of multiple families, as well as the integration of third families into multitude families and, of course, the creative employment of children. That means social policy. On this occasion, I would like to refer a reference to an argument that has been used in the last few days to establish the social solidarity benefit in your days. You did create the guaranteed social income, but it was pilot. It concerned only certain regions of the country at the beginning, with far less beneficiaries - thirty thousand in particular - who were being treated as bad by the administrative structure, while there were great delays in payments, which were up to twelve months. The social solidarity allowance, on the contrary, was transparent, with electronic procedures that we set up, rapid and regular payments, and concerned hundreds of thousands of our fellow citizens - six hundred and fifty thousand in particular - who were treated with dignity. Comparisons are catalytic and fortunately we didn't all lose our memory. Today's bill is also emblematic, because at last your announcement is being implemented, which has a positive sign, even though it is significantly modified, since the birth allowance will enter two instalments, which we do not agree and we have already tabled an amendment on the one-off payment, which we call on you to support. In conclusion, I would like to stress the following. The demographic, ladies and gentlemen, cannot be treated by fireworks. The implementation of an electoral announcement, even if it was flirting openly with the right-wing rhetoric for beneficiaries, is not compared to the targeted policy to support the demographic problem, which was very well reflected by the committee we set up and which gave us valuable conclusions. It is done with school meals that covered the needs of thousands of schools, with the inclusion of all children at school stations for the first time in 2019 thanks to the Voucher provision and the introduction of two-year compulsory pre-school education, a measure for which the political representatives of the New Democracy at all levels were initially opposed. This is social policy; long-term, duration and targeting; not with one-off payments suitable only for headlines and repository reviews from the media balls. Nor can it be done by the punishing attitude that is adopted and I am referring specifically to the loss of all the benefits that a family is entitled to if even a child loses one class at his school. Finally, I would like to inform basically those who are following us that SYRIZA, loyal to its logic of targeted demographic policy, has tabled an amendment to the organic integration of creative employment centres for children and children with disabilities into the administrative structure of the municipalities that are part of it. These structures with their employees fully support the Greek family. I would remind you that sixty-seven-and-a-half thousand children have joined the CSRs in 2019-20 and some four and a half thousand disabled people, and we must shield them even further, including them in the permanent municipal services, so that they can form another support mechanism for the family in our country. In conclusion, I would like to stress that social policy has never been your strongest point, since according to neoliberalism there is only society for you. With today's bill it turns out that you are consistent with this line, as in a positive measure you put asterisks, enter exceptions, make cuts, punish off offenders. Even here, as in the debate on the Constitution, on labour and taxation, the concept of social rights, total and inalienable, is causing you allergy. No surprise for us. The same applies to the Greek society that already experiences the frustration and deception of your great words. Thank you very much.

This allowance, as has been said, does not fall under any category of income, is exempted from any tax, levy or public contribution, is not seized, nor is it offset by established debts to public or credit institutions, and is not counted against total actual or imputable family income. The aim is to provide horizontal and almost universal provision under the following two conditions: The income below a certain limit and the permanent and legal residence on the territory. It is without doubt an issue of vital importance for the continuity of Hellenism as an autonomous and distinct national entity and cultural identity. If this thorny equation is added to the problem of illegal immigration, everyone realizes that very soon the coherent joints of the Greek society will be subjected to great compaction and will be tested very strongly. Engaging hypogenicity can only be seen as a national threat. Starting in 2011 where a negative birth balance had occurred, something that had to happen since the German occupation, the forecasts of the surveys cannot be simply alarming. It's more than arrogant. Just think that in 2050 Greece will number eight million inhabitants of whom 36% will be over 65, while no one can determine what path the migratory crisis will take and how quickly and efficiently we will achieve the multi-positional brand gain. The ageing population and the subsequent absence of new ageing potential, drugacts the economic development of the country, eliminates the insurance system and leads to the collapse of pensions. The Government of New Democracy in all of this responds positively and decisively and acts in intervention as long as the situation is still reversible. With a measure which forms part of the broader government's planning of maternity and fertility support policies, we actively support new couples, encourage births and shield the institution of the nuclear family, the backbone of our society. This allowance is not a communication firework. We don't score allowances to the right and the left to get electoral clientele. We want the citizens of this country to be independent and capable of producing their own wealth for themselves, not to fall and behave by a paternalist state. For us, it is a clear ideological choice with a specific political aim. It's not a submerged metro. It continues with an integrated policy framework that creates a favourable welfare environment for young Greeks and Greeks who are in childbearing age. I would remind you, ladies and gentlemen, that the government measures for day-to-day nurseries, the reduction of VAT on infants from 24% to 13%, the abolition of luxurious living tax on private vehicles of more than two thousand cubic metres, the increase in indirect tax exemption, and even with a provision of EUR 1 000 per minor child, the recent regulations in the education bill. There's more coming. A fragile national trunk that will be inspired by vital energy with a glance in the future is the basic prerequisite for jumping faster than wear. I am convinced of the vision of government policy in demographics and I join with all my forces in resolving this top political issue that directly affects the present and the future of our country. As Thucydides wrote, the great father of history, the city's walls are its people. We must therefore preserve these precious walls unreservedly and lovablely over time. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u195	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, my presence today at the Step of the House is very pleased, as we are discussing the introduction of a measure which will be of direct benefit to the core core of society, which is what is other than the family. The main aim of the measure is to harmonise the burden of giving birth to a child in the family budget, which as a mother I am well aware of. For this reason, the planned provision is expected to work beneficially for our country on multiple levels, as experience of the past has shown, aiming primarily at its demographic recovery. That is why I thought this measure was to be broadly accepted and not to be an excuse for pointless controversy. I was a little surprised by Mr Chakalotos when in one way, I did not understand what he tried to tell us. Come here to give birth, get the allowance and leave? What can I say? We are therefore invited to vote today on the introduction of a 2 000 euro allowance for every child born in Greece. The reasoning that led to this decision is the direct result of the downward trend of the birth index in our country. In fact, ELSTAT's measurements show the downward trend of births in Greece since 1981, while corresponding surveys have shown a drastic decline in families with more than two children in recent years, while at the same time increasing childbirth. This social phenomenon was particularly worrying in the decades of the crisis, as families with children were severely affected by the harsh fiscal policy. This situation has made Greek society aged and vulnerable. The workforce decreased, resulting in further contraction of any production power left. The gap between insured and retired persons in active employment has increased until about one employee maintains three pensioners, resulting in a reduction in contributions and a large number of funds become deficits. In the first place, however, subgenitality puts the country's own security at risk, when neighbouring Turkey, for example, presents ever-increasing trends in births with clear implications for its ever-increasing aggressive policy. The introduction of the birth allowance is a clear sign of social maturity and, of course, of justice. The aim of this is to strengthen the Greek family and to lay the foundations for the implementation of a modern demographic policy, as the New Democracy party sees it, in accordance with the constitutional order, which, on the one hand, serves the national interest and, on the other hand, highlights the undeniable European course of our country. We are fully aware, ladies and gentlemen, of the fact that the birth allowance is not going to be a panoramic of the birth rate in Greece. It will certainly be an incentive to recover, and it is worth mentioning here that when Kostas Karamanlis' government introduced in 2006 an equivalent third child's birth allowance and upwards, the next year births have increased by some four thousand, according to ELSTAT's figures. From a legal point of view now, particular attention has been paid to the conditions of granting. These are becoming strict and continuous in order to limit cases of abuse or unlawful exercise of the right, which is the result of the grant of benefits. The tax declaration criterion and the issue of a clearing agent are chosen as the safest and fairest condition for granting it, ensuring what else is the citizen's remuneration with the State. An income criterion is introduced, but calibrated in an objective way, depending on the economic status of each family, attributed to an equivalent family income. At the same time, for foreigners, the criterion of 12 years of permanent and legal residence in Greece is being set, of course, by guaranteeing social justice. In particular, it is essential to provide that the birth allowance becomes inappropriate and tax-free, even if there are outstanding debts of the beneficiary or regulated debts, completely separating social care from other administrative or sanctions. In this way, therefore, a safety net is being created for all those who really offer it in that country, making it a tangible financial aid to patients even today's family budgets. It is worth noting that the income criterion anticipated relates to the majority of families in Greece, the low and medium-sized groups, who have suffered the greatest pressure, of course, during the economic crisis. That is why the birth allowance is another measure to restore the unjust treatment that has been suffered mainly by SYRIZA's social policy. In any case, I must repeat that we are not ethers to believe that this provision will bring about a rapid increase in births. It is, however, a very important financial aid for the family, which we want and which we can support. In our criticism of another allowance we have also answered in the past about the opposition of the New Democracy to the support of a donation policy. However, until budgetary discipline allows for more and more permanent family support measures, we cannot completely abolish such measures. Ladies and gentlemen, the same draft law includes provision for extending child benefit to more social groups, promoting social justice and strengthening the social fabric. In this case too, however, we are looking further for a fair distribution of the allowance, introducing an additional allowance fee. In addition to the tax declaration, the child will now be required to receive a regular and uninterrupted student at a public school with continuous control of it. Its aim is to avoid phenomena such as the entry in children's schools which are not attending the courses, as well as other similar ones observed in the case of specific social groups and which have never been adequately addressed, for obvious reasons. [[At this point the end of the speaking time of the honourable Member is ringing at the end of the bell.]] Wait a minute, Mr. President.
