ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u213	Good evening, ladies and gentlemen. The demographic problem is evolving into one of the greatest threats to the future of the Greek nation. Estimates of the future are nightmareous. If our country continues at the same rate, in 2050 we will be around eight million and out of them, almost three million will be over-aged. The main cause for this situation is, of course, inheritability. 2011 was the first time a negative birth balance was recorded, i.e. the Greeks who died were more than those who were born. The economic crisis has certainly significantly exacerbated the problems, but it is not the sole reason for reducing these births. Among the main reasons for the reduction of the population can be said to be the cut-off from Greek traditions that often honoured luxury, the increase in the average age of marriage and as a result of childbirth, as well as the reduction in the birth rate of more children, consumerism, the increase in the cost of living and expenditure of children, the occupational employment of both parents, the modern patterns of living that often take more time to focus on private and family life, the abuses of modern lifestyles that are overconsumption, smoking, obesity and many of these cases also reduce fertility. In the New Democracy, we are fully aware of the criticality of the situation and are fighting for a comprehensive response to the causes, where this is possible on the part of the state. Obviously, we cannot demand that our fellow citizens have more children. But we can give them the motives in this direction. That's exactly what we do. Enhancement of fertility is not achieved with wishes. A political strategy is needed, a package of measures aimed at supporting children and the family. It is precisely such a policy that the Government of New Democracy plans from day one and implements, with financial support and tax relief for the family, measures to support the many families and families with disabilities, ensuring access to nurseries. In this context, with the proposed draft law of the Ministry of Labour and Social Affairs, the Greek state's subsidisation policy is being modernised. A birth allowance of EUR 2,000 is established for children born in Greece as from 1 January of this year and income criteria are set for the benefit in such a way that it is allocated to almost all beneficiaries. Special care shall be taken to support single-parent families. It is even worth noting that this allowance is exempted from any tax, levy or levy for the benefit of the public or third parties. It's not subject to confiscation. It shall not be offset by established public debts, legal entities governed by public law, TAs, insurance funds, etc. This is the first time that such a measure has finally been introduced. We're proud of this. A benefit does not solve all the problems I mentioned at the beginning of my speech. It does, however, make a significant contribution to supporting the country's modern demographic policy, making it an important incentive for future parents. It is not only the financial valuation of the allowance that is worthwhile, but also the message we send to the Greek society. Now the state is here, it is present and it supports the Greek family, not in words, but in actions. As in practice and in words, a responsible government working in the interests of all Greeks must speak. Ladies and gentlemen, the New Democracy's plan to deal with demographics is not limited to granting the allowance we are enacting today. It is complemented by access to nurseries, increasing the tax on each child, seeking better family and working life balance, reforms that bring investments and well paid jobs. At the same time, we are seeking to stop the outflow of the new Greeks abroad, as well as the return of those who left the difficult years of the crisis. This is our policy, a policy that supports the family, a policy for all Greeks and all Greeks, a policy with a view to better life. At the end of the four-year period Greece must be and will be a much better country than that we received. I invite you all to work together. We owe it to our children. We owe it to the next generations. Be well.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u234	Thank you, Mr President. Madam Minister, ladies and gentlemen, today's bill is, in my opinion, important. This declining course is found to start in the late 1980s, so the fertility index fell below 1.5, a decisive limit. In fact, experts point out that the society that has fallen below this threshold is difficult to return. The estimates are that our country's population by 2050 will be eight million to eight million five hundred thousand. Despite the corresponding bad predictions, there are countries such as France, England and the Scandinavian countries, which have significantly improved their fertility rates. It is necessary for Greece to motivate families to have more children and the first step is taking place today with the bill under discussion. The allowance of 2,000 euros per child born in Greece was a promise of the Prime Minister and it becomes a reality. Is the problem of the birth and ageing of the population in this way solved? The birth allowance is actually granted to all families, except for those who do not need it - a rate of about 1% - with a limit of 60,000 euros annually for the mother alone, 70,000 euros for the couple without children and 100,000 for the couple with three children. Very importantly, the allowance is not taxed at all and is not confiscated by any source, neither by public or credit institutions. The beneficiaries are Greek citizens, Greek citizens, European citizens, and citizens of third countries who have lived permanently and legally in Greece over the last 12 years. And why twelve years? The choice of the 12-year limit ensures that beneficiaries are families that have indeed chosen Greece as their country of permanent residence and have developed stable links here. In addition, it is of a demographic and not a foretaste. In addition to the arrangements for the birth allowance, the bill makes small, but key interventions as a whole in the field of pre-school policy. The tax-free and irresponsible treatment of households of mountainous and disadvantaged areas is being introduced. This allowance is received by families with a total income of 2.200 euros per year, namely families living with less than 200 euros a month in remote areas of Greece. Therefore, the new regulation is a imperative support for these people. The current government has firmly demonstrated that it is constantly trying to combat bureaucracy and simplify procedures for citizens' communication with the state. Almost all bills provide for provisions that move in this direction. Today's bill does the statement and registration of the birth of the child once and for all in the maternity hospital, with an obligation to inform all public services, without that being the case for a different procedure on the part of the parents. In the same context of simplification, each birth permit operation includes the Tax Register Number and Social Security Register Number. The citizens' facilitation and the fight against bureaucracy shall be acted upon and in accordance with the provision providing for electronic access to the services of the EPC, such as attestations, evidence of insurance information, etc. Finally, a social problem but also an educational problem is that there are families who do not comply with compulsory schooling for their children, so there is still a percentage of children who do not even complete primary school. The current arrangements relate to the payment of the child's allowance, the housing allowance, the minimum guaranteed income, which was recently introduced by the Constitutional Review, as well as the actual schooling of minors in a family. In conclusion, the government constantly proves that it closely monitors the needs of the citizens and stands alongside the social groups that need support. Thank you.

ParlaMint-GR_2020-01-28-S1-commons.u236	Thank you, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, in a country where the unborn child is defenseless and the demographic problem has a nightmareous national dimension, bills as they see it as oasis to the unhospitable desert of our time and offer smiles to everyone. The commitment that was the crown of this strategy is to support EUR 2 000 for every child born on 1 January 2020 in Greece, even introducing very extensive income criteria, so that this aid can be obtained by the vast majority of the new Greek families and not, of course, by those who do not need it, as will be analysed below. However, this is only one of the measures aimed at restoring the ingenuity, which poses a significant threat to further declining the population in Greece, as is apparent from the statistics of the Greek Parliament. In 2020, birth reduction and ageing populations are expected to continue. Increasingly fewer children will meet in the playgrounds and in school units, while, on the contrary, the meeting places of the elderly will grow. Unfortunately, if the country continues at the same rate, without drastic measures being taken to resolve the demographic problem, which is agitating our country, in 2050, we will barely reach eight million, of which almost three million will be old. And this is a conservative scenario, because it is likely that new couples will continue not to have many children because of economic difficulties and a series of other endogenous and exogenous factors. With a view, therefore, to halting the birth rate and addressing the demographic problem, a number of measures have already been taken by the present government and even more will be taken in the future. I mention attributes the tax-free limit for each child to be increased by EUR 1,000, but also the subjection to the low VAT rate for all baby and baby species for young mothers. As far as this bill is concerned, the allowance will be paid in two equal instalments of EUR 1,000, the first instalment of which will be paid in the last working week of next month of birth and the other EUR 1,000 after six months. It will be granted to the mother or father or other person exercising custody of the child, if he or she is legally and permanently resident in the Greek territory and has the status of Greek citizen or foreigner, citizen of a Member State of the European Union or citizen of a third country residing permanently in Greece for the last 12 years before the child's birth. Exceptionally, for children born in Greece in 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, the allowance is granted if the mother of that third-country citizen resides permanently in the Greek territory from 2012 onwards. The beneficiary of the allowance is the mother, even if it does not belong to one of these categories, since it resides legally and permanently in Greece and if the father belongs to one of the above categories. I note that this income allowance is paid to 99% of the citizens. In particular, the bill sets out its allocation based on the equivalent family income, the limit of which is set at EUR 40,000 per annum, as shown by the tax declaration. An equivalent family income shall be defined as the total actual or imputable income from each source of domestic and foreign origin before tax after deduction of social security contributions, with the exception of allowances which are not counted on the taxable income of all members of the family, divided by the equivalence scale. The draft law expressly describes the level of equivalence depending on the family. It is also worth noting that the allowance is not regarded as income and is exempted from any tax, levy, levy or reservation in favour of the public or third parties, including the special solidarity contribution, while neither is confiscated nor offset for debts to the public and banks. The application will be submitted either to the civil registry information system subsystem for the birth declaration or directly to the OPEK system for the birth allowance. In addition to the introduction for the first time of the birth allowance, however, this draft law also updates the conditions for granting four more allowances. In particular, with regard to child benefit, housing and minimum guaranteed income due to the need for budgetary stability, the 12-year stay for citizens of third countries not from a country of the European Union and not subject to a special protection scheme is laid down as an additional criterion, and the compulsory schooling of children of the benefiting families is laid down as an additional criterion, since these three allowances are allowances for the ultimate purpose of raising the standard of living. In addition, as regards the benefit of mountain and disadvantaged areas, the tax-free and incompatible aid is envisaged as a measure to safeguard the income of beneficiary families, given that adverse living conditions in these areas generate additional costs. In addition to other arrangements, the terms and conditions of the CRL shall be specified in this draft law and shall be deleted by the CRL for legal and natural persons by 31 January of the following calendar year. In particular, such debts shall be written off from twenty years after the year of the attestation, provided that the total amount of the debts to the ECA does not exceed EUR 30 per debtor, and that arrears not exceeding EUR 10 are entered. Noteworthy and the provision of Article 28 of the draft law, whereby the possibility of developing e-government transactions at Level 4 is established in the context of the digital transformation of the NCA and allows insured persons to trade electronically with the services of the ECAI. To conclude, I would like to stress that the sole objective of this bill is to support the Greek family and to improve the lives of all citizens, so its vote is the duty of all of us. Thank you very much.

Demographic data is unfortunately producing negative developments in all sectors. According to a European Commission report on population ageing, data for Greece are dramatic. Today the population is about 11 million. In 2060 it is planned that it will be reduced to 8.6 million. In combination with the further increase in life expectancy, our country is largely transformed into a society of old age. The rate of the child's population up to 14 years will decline from 14.6%, which was in 2013, to 12.9% in 2060, while the working solar group of the population from 15 to sixty-four years will decrease from 65% to 54% in the respective years. The proportion of the elderly will rise from 20.3% in 2013 to 33% in 2060. Of these, about half, i.e. 46%, will be over eighty years old. Ladies and gentlemen, there is a constant drop in birth rates in our country at levels below the European average. This coupled with the fact that more and more people decide to have a child at an older age means that the number of children in most families does not exceed two and thus the population of the country constantly shrinks. In addition, the decline in the birth rate is linked to the insurance system, since birth reduction and at the same time ageing of the population means that the workforce is reduced and pensioners are increased. The figures for Epirus do not differ from the general picture. According to ELSTAT, deaths are, unfortunately, far superior to births. For the year 2018 in Epirus, births amounted to two thousand two hundred twenty-two and deaths three thousand nine hundred seventy-seven. The bill we are voting on introduces a birth allowance of EUR 2 000 for the first time. It also facilitates access to finance for the municipalities of the country, with the aim of operating more kindergartens to ensure the place for all the families in need. Changes are also taking place in the existing child and rental allowances, as are the social solidarity allowance, which is renamed to a minimum guaranteed income. Our aim is for the birth allowance to be horizontal and almost universal in 99% of the Greek population. For this reason, access to the allowance is conditional on permanent and legal residence in the country. As far as the criteria for residence are concerned, we have followed the best European practices. Immigrants need to be permanently resident in the country for twelve years to receive the child and rental allowance, and compulsory schooling for young children of the family is laid down as an additional criterion. Ladies and gentlemen, we need seriousness, a national strategy, to insist on our policy objectives over time.
